Durable/replay documentation and systems papers support implementation-specific guarantees. The strongest scoped result cannot be transferred to uncooperative external actions.</p></dd></dl></dd><dt>Decision or consumer</dt><dd><p>Workflow-code author and runtime operator.</p></dd><dt>Actor and authority boundary</dt><dd><p>Replay consistency is not permission to repeat an external action or proof that its returned value was correct.</p></dd><dt>Communication or interaction requirement</dt><dd><p>Expose which values were recorded and which computations are re-executed.</p></dd><dt>Observable postcondition</dt><dd><p>Replaying a supported history under compatible code preserves the required command sequence.</p></dd><dt>Known failure modes</dt><dd><ol><li><p>Replay emits a different command sequence or repeats nondeterministic work as if it were pure control logic.</p></li><li><p>Abstraction leaks, prohibited APIs and recorded bad inputs; deterministic wrong behaviour remains wrong.</p></li></ol></dd><dt>Important criticisms</dt><dd><p><a href="../../data/relationships/shared/d8.md#r-d8f0877ca4a86860568351336fdc0d6d6c7023f53c6d4eb93896c46d0b8095b6">Complete shared IMPORTANT CRITICISMS · d8f0877ca4a8</a></p></dd><dt>How the property evolved</dt><dd><p>DOMAIN_SPECIFIC: replay constraints refine durable execution but are not a definition of all workflow management.</p></dd><dt>Mature or evolved form</dt><dd><p>Keep replay-sensitive decisions within the deterministic contract and isolate or record nondeterministic inputs and effects through the supported interfaces. Retain only for replay-based implementations and clearly delimited deterministic components.</p></dd><dt>Ceremony vs property</dt><dd><dl><dt>Protected function</dt><dd><p>Replay emits a different command sequence or repeats nondeterministic work as if it were pure control logic.</p></dd><dt>Minimal adequate alternative</dt><dd><p>A state-machine snapshot engine, explicit persisted state or a short transaction may not require this particular programming discipline.</p></dd><dt>Fuller form trigger</dt><dd><p>The selected runtime rebuilds orchestration by replay.</p></dd><dt>Consumer</dt><dd><p>Workflow-code author and runtime operator.</p></dd><dt>Cost of simplification</dt><dd><p>Removing the mechanism is acceptable only while the cheap path still establishes this claim: Replaying a supported history under compatible code preserves the required command sequence.</p></dd><dt>Retirement or omission</dt><dd><p>Retire or omit the extra machinery when the trigger, consumer or relevant dependency disappears, after settling/transferring the outstanding work or evidence that still relies on it.
